\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }1<|A_{1}|^{-1+\delta^{2}}\leq|A_{2}|^{-1}\leq|A_{1}|^{-1-\delta^{2}}<\ell_{{}_{\scriptstyle 1}}^{{}^{\scriptstyle 1+\delta^{2}}},

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\ }\ell_{p_{1},p_{2}}{\scriptscriptstyle}:={\scriptscriptstyle}|X_{2}|\cdot|A_{1}|^{-\delta^{2}}{\scriptscriptstyle}+{\scriptscriptstyle}(|x_{{2}}|{\scriptscriptstyle}+{\scriptscriptstyle}|x_{{2}}{\scriptscriptstyle}+{\scriptscriptstyle}y_{{2}}|){\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{2}^{3}{\scriptscriptstyle}\geq{\scriptscriptstyle}1{\scriptscriptstyle}+{\scriptscriptstyle}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{2}^{2},

Now we divide the proof of Proposition

Lemma 3.27

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(i)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{tX1-is=}}}}{{=}}\frac{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}}{1+\delta}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}

(3.138)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =(1-\delta){\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}+({\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}})^{-\ell_{0}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{A1-A2-cond}\,(a)}}}{{\leq}}\gamma_{1}({\textbf{{y}}}):=(1-\delta){\textbf{{y}}}+{\textbf{{y}}}^{-\ell_{0}}\mbox{ \ [up to $O(\delta)^{2}\scriptscriptstyle\,$]},\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\ }{\textbf{{y}}}:={\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}.\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

Noting from
(
3.119
) (a), (
3.28
) (i) that the last term
in the first line of
(
3.138
) (i) is
\leq 1
[up to
O(\delta)^{2}\scriptscriptstyle\,
], we must have
{\textbf{{y}}}\geq 1+\delta
[up to
O(\delta)^{2}\scriptscriptstyle\,
].
Regarding
\gamma_{1}({\textbf{{y}}})
as a function on
y
, by solving
\frac{d\gamma_{1}}{d{\textbf{{y}}}}=0
, we see that
the function
\gamma_{1}({\textbf{{y}}})
is
strictly decreasing when
1\leq{\textbf{{y}}}\leq{\textbf{{y}}}_{0}
and strictly increasing when
{\textbf{{y}}}\geq{\textbf{{y}}}_{0}
, where

{\textbf{{y}}}_{0}=\big(\frac{\ell_{0}}{1-\delta}\big)^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}}
.
Since
\gamma_{1}(2-\delta_{0}^{2})=2-\delta_{0}^{2}+O(\delta_{0})^{100}<3
[noting that
2^{-\ell_{0}}\ll\delta_{0}^{100}
and
\delta\ll\delta_{0}\scriptscriptstyle\,
],
in order for (
3.138
) (i) to hold, we must have (i) below, and
so the second term in (
3.138
) (i) is an
O(\delta_{0})^{100}
element, which gives (ii) below. Therefore, we have,
up to
O(\delta)^{1}
[noting that we can conduct the following computations up to
O(\delta)^{1}
as
\delta\ll\delta_{0}

and recalling that
\log(\cdot)
is the natural logarithmic function],

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }{\textbf{{y}}}>2-\delta_{0}^{2},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {\rm(ii)\ }2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(i)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}-\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}={\textbf{{y}}}+O(\delta_{0})^{100},

(3.139)

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)\ }{\textbf{{y}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(i),\,(ii)}}}{{=}}2+O(\delta_{0})^{100},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {\rm(iv)\ }{\textbf{{y}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{x2-00ro4m4m4-}\,(ii)}}}{{=}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{We0o0o0o}\,(i)}}}{{\leq}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{-1},

\displaystyle{\rm(v)\ }{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{A1-A2-cond}\,(a)}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}={\textbf{{y}}}=2+O(\delta_{0})^{100},

\displaystyle{\rm(vi)\ }\frac{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{tX1-is=}}}}{{=}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{-2-\delta_{0}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{x2-00ro4m4m4-}\,(ii)}}}{{=}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{-2-2\delta_{0}}{\textbf{{y}}}^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(iv)}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{y}}}^{1+2\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\phantom{{\rm(vi)\ }\ \ }\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(iii)}}}{{=}}2^{1+2\delta_{0}}+O(\delta_{0})^{100}=2+4\log(2)\delta_{0}+O(\delta_{0})^{2}.

The importance of the fact in (
3.139
) (vi) that it is a number strictly bigger than
2

is that we can use it and the
expression of
A_{2}
in (
3.135
) to obtain, up to
O(\delta)^{1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+O(\delta_{0})^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(iii),\,(iv)}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{cont-B2}}}}{{=}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{We0o0o0o++1}}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}}-2\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(v),\,(vi)}}}{{\geq}}\Big(2+O(\delta_{0})^{100}\Big)^{\ell_{0}}\Big(4\log(2)\delta_{0}+O(\delta_{0})^{2}\Big)\gg 2,

(3.140)

which is a contradiction.
This proves the first inequality of
(
3.28
) (iv).

Next assume the first inequality of (
3.28
) (iv) does not hold. Then
by (
3.3
) (iii),

\displaystyle\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}=(1-\delta){\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2+\delta_{0}}.

(3.141)

Using the second expression of
A_{2}
in (
3.135
) and the fact in (
3.118
)
that
{\textbf{{a}}}_{2}={\textbf{{a}}}_{1}
[up to
O(\delta)^{2}\scriptscriptstyle\,
],
we can compute the following, up to
O(\delta)^{2}
,

\displaystyle 2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{We0o0o0o++1}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{2}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{cont-B2},\,\eqref{111---tX1-is=111}}}}{{=}}(1-\delta){\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{\delta_{0}}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}\mbox{ \ [up to $O(\delta)^{2}\scriptscriptstyle\,$]}.\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

(3.142)

Observing that
(
3.142
) is exactly the same as the first line in
(
3.138
) (i),
we can thus use the same arguments after (
3.138
) to show
that we have
(
3.139
) (i)–(v), and further, using (
3.141
),
exactly as in (
3.139
) (vi), we have
\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}={\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{-2-\delta_{0}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{-1}\geq 2+4\log(2)\delta_{0}+O(\delta_{0})^{2}
. Then
as in (
3.3
), we can use the definition of
A_{1}
in
(
3.116
) (i) to obtain, up to
O(\delta)^{1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+O(\delta_{0})^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{uuuauyyY}\,(iii),\,(iv)}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(i)}}}{{\geq}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}\Big(\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}-2\Big)\geq\Big(2+O(\delta_{0})^{100}\Big)^{\ell_{0}}\Big(4\log(2)\delta_{0}+O(\delta_{0})^{2}\Big)\gg 2,

which is again a contradiction. This proves the last inequality of (
3.28
) (iv).

Finally, (
3.28
) (v) follows from (
3.15
) (i) together with
(
3.25
) (i), (
3.28
) (i)–(iii).
This completes the prove of Lemma
3.28
.

\Box

Lemma 3.29
.

We have
\overline{V}_{0}=V_{0}
.

Proof.
Let
(p_{{1}},p_{{2}})\in\overline{V}_{0}
.
Using notations in (
3.130
), we already see from
(
3.28
) that all strict inequalities in
(
3.7
) (c)
[or (
3.25
) (iii) ] hold. In particular,
(p_{{1}},p_{{2}})\in S_{2}
(cf. proof of Lemma
3.9
).

By Lemma
3.9
, first assume equality occurs in the first inequality of (
3.8
) (a).
Using notations in (
3.130
), we have, where
T_{2}
is defined in (
3.130
),

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}={\textbf{{a}}}_{2}=1,\ \ \ \ \ {\rm(ii)\ }{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{ImMpP}\,(i)}}}{{>}}1.

(3.143)

Then we can use the definitions of
A_{1},A_{2}
in (
3.116
) to obtain

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!{\rm(i)\ }2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(i)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{1bab-that}}}}{{<}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}+1,\ \ \implies\ \ \ {\rm(ii)\ }\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}<1,

(3.144)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!{\rm(iii)\,}2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{{\textbf{{a}}}_{2}{\textbf{{z}}}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}+1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{1+1bab-that}\,(ii),\,\eqref{1bab-that}}}}{{<}}1+\frac{{\textbf{{z}}}}{{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}},\ \ \implies\ \ \ {\rm(iv)\ }1\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{1bab-that}\,(ii)}}}{{<}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}<{\textbf{{z}}}.\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

By (
3.14
), we also have
{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}=\alpha_{0}^{-1}\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}<\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}<1<{\textbf{{z}}}
.
We obtain,
where the first equality follows by noting from (
3.6
), (
3.7
), (
3.130
) that

|x_{1}|={\textbf{{x}}}_{1}{\textbf{{k}}},\,|x_{2}|={\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{k}}}
,

\displaystyle\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{|x_{1}|,|x_{2}|}=\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}{\textbf{{k}}},{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{k}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{wePPPP1+}}}}{{<}}\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{{\textbf{{z}}}{\textbf{{k}}},{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}{\textbf{{k}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{wePPPP1}}}}{{\leq}}\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{{\textbf{{z}}}{\textbf{{k}}},{\textbf{{z}}}{\textbf{{k}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{AssG}}}}{{\leq}}{\textbf{{z}}}\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{{\textbf{{k}}},{\textbf{{k}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{TaKa},\,\eqref{SimMMSMS}}}}{{=}}|x_{2}+y_{2}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{Ak=1}}}}{{\leq}}\mbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\!}$\Large$\gamma$}_{|x_{1}|,|x_{2}|},

(3.145)

which is
a contradiction.
This proves that the first strict inequality of (
3.25
) (i) holds for
(p_{1},p_{2})
.

Next assume in (
3.3
) (i),
equality occurs in the last inequality, i.e.,
{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}=|A_{1}|=\delta_{1}
, and
{\textbf{{a}}}_{2}=\delta_{1}+O(\delta)^{1}

by (
3.118
).

First assume
\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}\leq\delta_{1}^{\delta_{0}}
. Then by recalling that
\delta\ll\delta_{1}\ll\delta_{0}
, up to
O(\delta)^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }\Big|2-\frac{1}{\tilde{X}_{1}}\Big|\geq\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}^{-1}-2\geq\ell_{1}^{\delta_{0}}-2>1,

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\ }\delta_{1}={\textbf{{a}}}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{LetNSoOP----1-re-give}\,(i)}}}{{=}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}\Big|2-\frac{1}{\tilde{X}_{1}}\Big|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{ImMpP}\,(i)}}}{{\geq}}\Big|2-\frac{1}{\tilde{X}_{1}}\Big|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{Mndndnpp}\,(i)}}}{{>}}1,

(3.146)

which is a contradiction.
Thus
\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}>\delta_{1}^{\delta_{0}}
. Then
\frac{\tilde{\textbf{{x}}}_{1}}{{\textbf{{a}}}_{1}^{2}}\geq\ell_{1}^{2-\delta_{0}}
, and
up to
O(\delta)^{1}
,

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }\Big|2-\frac{\tilde{X}_{1}}{A_{1}^{2}}\Big|>\ell_{1}^{2-\delta_{0}}-2>1,\ \ \ \ \ {\rm(ii)\ }\delta_{1}={\textbf{{a}}}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{We0o0o0o++1}}}}{{>}}{\textbf{{x}}}_{2}^{\ell_{0}}\Big|2-\frac{\widetilde{X}_{1}}{A_{1}^{2}}\Big|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}^{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{ImMpP}\,(i),\,\eqref{Mndndnpp++}\,(i)}}}{{\geq}}1,\!\!\!\!

(3.147)

which is again a contradiction.
This proves that the last strict inequality of (
3.25
) (i) holds for
(p_{1},p_{2})
.

The above with (
3.3
) shows that (
3.25
) is satisfied by
(p_{1},p_{2})
. Thus (
3.7
) is satisfied by
(p_{1},p_{2})
,
which implies that
(p_{1},p_{2})\in V_{0}
. This proves that
\overline{V}_{0}=V_{0}
.

\Box

Now Proposition
3.7

follows from Lemmas
3.27
–
3.29
together with the following facts.
When
(p_{1},p_{2})\in V_{0}
, by (
3.28
),
x_{1},x_{2}
are bounded,
thus
V_{0}
is bounded by Proposition
2.22
. Further by (
3.7
), (
3.5
) (iii), (
3.119
), (
3.28
), we see that

x_{1},x_{2},x_{2}+y_{2}\neq 0
, i.e., (
3.18
) holds.
This completes the proof.
\Box

Appendix A
Another proof of surjectivity of
\pi_{1}

To see our original idea on how to obtain the surjectivity of
\pi_{1}
, for your reference, below we would like to present our original proof of surjectivity of
\pi_{1}
by simply using fundamental mathematical methods.

Assume conversely
\pi_{1}
is not surjective. Fix any
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\backslash\pi_{1}(V)
, and define

L_{p_{1},p_{2}}=|x_{1}-\xi_{1}|^{2}+|x_{2}-\xi_{2}|^{2}\mbox{ for }(p_{1},p_{2})=\big((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})\big)\in V.

(A.1)

Then
L_{p_{1},p_{2}}>0
for
(p_{1},p_{2})\in V
. Fix any
(\bar{p}_{1},\bar{p}_{2})\in V
, and denote

V_{3}=\{(p_{1},p_{2})\in V\,|\,L_{p_{1},p_{2}}\leq L_{\bar{p}_{1},\bar{p}_{2}}\}.

(A.2)

Then
V_{3}\neq\emptyset.
By (
A.1
) and Theorem
1.2
,
V_{3}
is compact in
\mathbb{C}^{4}
. Then the following proposition (which says that the continuous function
L_{p_{1},p_{2}}
does not have the minimal value on the compact set
V_{3}
) immediately gives a contradiction, which proves the surjectivity of
\pi_{1}
.

Proposition A.1
.

For any
(p_{1},p_{2})\in V_{3}
, in every neighborhood of
(p_{{1}},p_{{2}})
there exists
(q_{1},q_{2})=\big((\dot{x}_{1},\dot{y}_{1}),(\dot{x}_{2},\dot{y}_{2})\big)\in V_{3}
such that

L_{q_{1},q_{2}}<L_{p_{1},p_{2}}.

(A.3)

Proof.
Let
(p_{{1}},p_{{2}})=\big((x_{{1}},y_{{1}}),(x_{{2}},y_{{2}})\big)\in V
and set
(and define
G_{{1}},G_{{2}}
similarly)

\displaystyle F_{{1}}=F(x_{{1}}+\alpha_{{1}}x,y_{{1}}+y)-F(x_{1},y_{1}),\ \,\ \ F_{{2}}=F(x_{{2}}+\alpha_{{2}}x,y_{{2}}+y)-F(x_{2},y_{2}),\mbox{ \ where}

(A.4)

\displaystyle\alpha_{{1}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\mbox{if }x_{{1}}=\xi_{{1}},\\[0.0pt]
x_{{1}}-\xi_{{1}}&\mbox{else},\end{array}\right.\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \,\ \alpha_{{2}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\mbox{if }x_{{2}}=\xi_{{2}},\\[0.0pt]
x_{{2}}-\xi_{{2}}&\mbox{else.}\end{array}\right.

Define
q_{{1}},q_{{2}}
accordingly [cf. (
3.42
) and (
3.27
) ],

q_{{1}}:=(\dot{x}_{{1}},\dot{y}_{{1}})=(x_{{1}}+\alpha_{{1}}s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},y_{{1}}+t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}),\ \ \ \ \ q_{{2}}:=(\dot{x}_{{2}},\dot{y}_{{2}})=(x_{{2}}+\alpha_{{2}}u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},y_{{2}}+v{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}).

(A.10)

We need to choose
(q_{1},q_{2})
to be in
V
[then it is automatically in
V_{3}
when (
A.3
) holds].

Let
A_{1}
be the unique invertible
2\times 2
complex matrix such that for Jacobian pair

(\bar{F}_{i},\bar{G}_{i}):=(F_{i},G_{i})A_{1}
for
i=1,2
,

(A.11)

the linear parts of
\bar{F}_{1},\bar{G}_{1},\bar{F}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}
, denoted as
\bar{F}_{1}^{\rm lin},\bar{G}_{1}^{\rm lin},\bar{F}_{2}^{\rm lin},\bar{G}_{1}^{\rm lin}
, are of the following forms,

\bar{F}_{1}^{\rm lin}=x,\ \ \bar{G}_{1}^{\rm lin}=y,\ \ \ \ \ \bar{F}_{2}^{\rm lin}=ax+by,\ \ \bar{G}_{2}^{\rm lin}=cx+dy,

(A.12)

for some
a,b,c,d\in\mathbb{C}
with
ad-bc=1
.
Note from (
A.4
), (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) that the equation (
3.28
) is equivalent to the equation

\big(\bar{F}_{1}(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}),\bar{G}_{1}(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})\big)=\big(\bar{F}_{2}(u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},v{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}),\bar{G}_{2}(u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},v{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})\big).

(A.13)

Then we can solve from (
A.13
) to obtain

s=au+bv+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{1}.

(A.14)

Note that
(x_{{1}},x_{{2}})\neq(\xi_{{1}},\xi_{{2}})
by the assumption.
First suppose
x_{{1}}\neq\xi_{{1}},\,x_{{2}}\neq\xi_{{2}}
(then
\alpha_{{1}}=x_{{1}}-\xi_{{1}},\,\alpha_{{2}}=x_{{2}}-\xi_{{2}}
).
In this case, by (
A.1
), (
A.3
), we need to choose
u,v
such that,

C_{{1}}:=\beta_{{1}}|1+s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}|^{2}+\beta_{{2}}|1+u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}|^{2}-(\beta_{{1}}+\beta_{{2}})<0,

(A.15)

where

\beta_{{1}}=|x_{{1}}-\xi_{{1}}|^{2}>0,\,\beta_{{2}}=|x_{{2}}-\xi_{{2}}|^{2}>0
.
Using (
A.14
) in (
A.15
), we immediately see (by comparing the coefficients of
{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{1}
) that if
b\neq 0
or
a\neq-{\beta_{{1}}}{\beta_{{2}}^{-1}}
, then we have a solution for (
A.15
). Thus assume

b=0,\ \ \ \ \ \ a=-{\beta_{{2}}}{\beta_{{1}}^{-1}}\in\mathbb{R}_{\neq 0}.

(A.16)

Then we can write

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }\bar{F}_{{1}}=\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i\geq 2}a_{i}y^{i}+x\Big(1+\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}\hat{a}_{i}y^{i}\Big)+\cdots,\ \ \bar{F}_{{2}}=\sum\limits_{i\geq 2}b_{i}z^{i}+ax\Big(1+\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}\hat{b}_{i}z^{i}\Big)+\cdots$},

(A.17)

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\,}\bar{G}_{{1}}=y+\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i\geq 2}c_{i}y^{i}+x\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}c^{\prime}_{i}y^{i}+\cdots,\ \ \ \ \ \bar{G}_{{2}}=z+\sum\limits_{i\geq 2}d_{i}z^{i}+x\sum\limits_{i\geq 1}d^{\prime}_{i}z^{i}+\cdots,\ z=cx+dy,$\!\!\!\!\!}

for some
a_{i},\hat{a}_{i},b_{i},\hat{b}_{i},c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},d_{i},d^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
, and where we regard
\bar{F}_{{2}},\bar{G}_{{2}}
as polynomials on
x,z
and we omit terms with
x
-degree
\geq 2
.

Lemma A.2
.

There exists some
i\geq 2
such that
(a_{i},c_{i})\neq(b_{i},d_{i})
.

Proof.
Denote
(\bar{F},\bar{G})=(F,G)A_{1}
, and we use the same symbols with a bar to denote the associated elements corresponding to the pair
(\bar{F},\bar{G})
. Then by definition of Keller maps, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}(p)=\big(\bar{F}(p),\bar{G}(p)\big)=\big(F(p),G(p)\big)A_{1}=\sigma(p)A_{1}\mbox{ for }p\in\mathbb{C}^{2}.

(A.18)

Assume
(a_{i},c_{i})=(b_{i},d_{i})
for
i\geq 2
. Then by the bar version of (
A.4
), we obtain (and the like for
\bar{G}
),

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\bar{F}(x_{{1}},y_{{1}}+{\textbf{{k}}})=\bar{F}_{{1}}\big|_{(x,y)=(0,{\textbf{{k}}})}=\bar{F}_{{2}}\big|_{(x,z)=(0,{\textbf{{k}}})}=\bar{F}(x_{{2}},y_{{2}}+d^{-1}{\textbf{{k}}}),

(A.19)

i.e.,
\bar{\sigma}(\hat{p}_{{1}})=\bar{\sigma}(\hat{p}_{{2}})
and so
\sigma(\hat{p}_{{1}})=\sigma(\hat{p}_{{2}})
by
(
A.18
) for
all
{\textbf{{k}}}\gg 1
, where

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{{1}}:=(\hat{x}_{{1}},\hat{y}_{{1}})=(x_{{1}},y_{{1}}+{\textbf{{k}}}),\ \ \ \ \hat{p}_{{2}}:=(\hat{x}_{{2}},\hat{y}_{{2}})=(x_{{2}},y_{{2}}+d^{-1}{\textbf{{k}}}).

(A.20)

Since
p_{{1}}\neq p_{{2}}
, we have
\hat{p}_{{1}}\neq\hat{p}_{{2}}
when
{\textbf{{k}}}\gg 1
, i.e.,
(\hat{p}_{{1}},\hat{p}_{{2}})\in V
.
Then (
A.20
) gives that
{h}_{\hat{p}_{{1}},\hat{p}_{{2}}}\sim_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textbf{{k}}}\,}{\textbf{{k}}}
and
|\hat{y}_{{2}}|\sim_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textbf{{k}}}\,}{\textbf{{k}}}\succ_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textbf{{k}}}\,}{h}_{{}_{\scriptstyle\hat{p}_{{1}},\hat{p}_{{2}}}}^{{}^{\scriptstyle\frac{m}{m+1}}}
, a contradiction with (
2.82
).

\Box

Lemma A.3
.

Let
i_{0}\geq 2
be the minimal number satisfying Lemma
A.2
.
We can assume

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }a_{i}=0,\,i=2,...,2i_{0},\ {\rm(ii)\ }b_{i}=0,\,i=2,...,2i_{0},\ {\rm(iii)\ }c_{i}=d_{i},\,i=2,...,i_{0}-1,\ c_{i_{0}}\neq d_{i_{0}}.

(A.21)

Proof.
To solve
s
from (
A.13
), we can replace
\bar{F}_{j}
by
\bar{F}_{j}+\sum_{i=2}^{2i_{0}}\beta_{i}\bar{G}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\,}i}_{j}
for some
\beta_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
and
j={1},{2}

(observe that
i_{0}
is still the minimal number satisfying Lemma
A.2
after the replacement
by considering either
c_{i_{0}}\neq d_{i_{0}}
or
c_{i_{0}}=d_{i_{0}},\,a_{i_{0}}\neq b_{i_{0}}
), thanks to the term
y
in
\bar{G}_{{1}}
, we can then suppose (

) (i) holds.
Assume

for some

. Take minimal such

.
Setting [noting from (

) that this amounts to setting

,

in

, and setting

in

, and letting

to solve

and regarding

as new variables,
by (

) (i), we have

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\bar{F}_{{2}}|_{(x,z)=(\check{u}{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}^{k},w{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}})}{\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}(b_{k}w^{k}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}a\check{u}){\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{k}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{k+1}{\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}\bar{F}_{{1}}(s{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}},t{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}){\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{k+1}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}\Big(1{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}O(t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{1}\Big){\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}O(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{2}.\!\!\!\!\!\!

(A.23)

This shows that we have
s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}=O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{k}
and the right-hand side of (

s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{k+1}

in this case. Therefore, we can assume

\Box

Lemma A.4
.

We have

\hat{a}_{i}=\hat{b}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq i_{0}-2
and
\kappa_{0}:=\hat{b}_{i_{0}-1}-\hat{a}_{i_{0}-1}=i_{0}(c_{i_{0}}-d_{i_{0}})\neq 0
.

Proof.
For
1\leq i\leq i_{0}-1
, by (
A.17
) we have,

\hat{a}_{i}+\sum\limits_{{1}\leq j<i}(j+1)\hat{a}_{j-i}c_{j+1}+(i+1)c_{i+1}={\rm C_{oeff}}\big(J(\bar{F}_{{1}},\bar{G}_{{1}}),x^{0}y^{i}\big)=0
,

\hat{b}_{i}+\sum\limits_{{1}\leq j<i}(j+1)\hat{b}_{j-i}d_{j+1}+(i+1)d_{i+1}=\displaystyle\frac{{\rm C_{oeff}}\big(J(\bar{F}_{{2}},\bar{G}_{{2}}),x^{0}y^{i}\big)}{ad}=0
.

(A.25)

Induction on

i
for
1\leq i\leq i_{0}
, we obtain the lemma by (
A.21
) (iii).
\Box

Now we set,

\displaystyle u=u_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{i_{0}-1},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ v=d^{-1}(w-cu),

(A.26)

for
u_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{\neq 0}
. As in (
A.23
), one can see that
s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}=O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i_{0}}
.
Thus by (
A.17
) (ii), we have

\displaystyle\bar{G}_{{1}}(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}){\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i=2}^{i_{0}}$}c_{i}(t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i_{0}+1}{\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}\bar{G}_{{2}}|_{(x,z)=(u_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}^{i_{0}},w{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}})}{\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}w{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i=2}^{i_{0}}$}d_{i}(w{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i}+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i_{0}+1}.

(A.27)

Hence
t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}=w{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i_{0}}
. Using this and (

) (i), we obtain,

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{{1}}(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}){\scriptstyle}={\scriptstyle}s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}\Big(1{\scriptstyle}+{\scriptstyle}\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i=1}^{i_{0}-1}$}\hat{a}_{i}(t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i}\Big){\scriptstyle}+{\scriptstyle}O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{2i_{0}}

\displaystyle\phantom{F_{{1}}(s{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}},t{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})}{\scriptstyle}={\scriptstyle}\bar{F}_{{2}}|_{(x,z)=(u_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}^{i_{0}},w{{\small\textbf{{\footnotesize$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}})}{\scriptstyle}={\scriptstyle}au_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{i_{0}}\Big(1{\scriptstyle}+{\scriptstyle}\mbox{$\sum\limits_{i=1}^{i_{0}-1}$}\hat{b}_{i}(w{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{i}\Big)+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{2i_{0}},

(A.28)

which with Lemma

gives that

.
Thus

\displaystyle C_{0}=\beta_{{1}}\Big|1-\beta_{{2}}\beta_{{1}}^{-1}u_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{i_{0}}(1+\kappa_{{1}}w^{i_{0}-1}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{i_{0}-1})+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{2i_{0}}\Big|^{2}+\beta_{{2}}|1+u_{1}{\textbf{{i}}}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{i_{0}}|^{2}-\beta_{{1}}-\beta_{{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{C_{0}}=2\beta_{{2}}u_{1}(\kappa_{{1}}w^{i_{0}-1})_{\rm im}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}^{2i_{0}-1}+O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}})^{2i_{0}},

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\,}C_{2}{\scriptstyle\!}={\scriptstyle\!}\kappa^{\prime}_{{1}}\big|1{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\tilde{\alpha}u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\tilde{\alpha}_{{1}1}u^{2}{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}^{2}\big|{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}|1{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}|{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\kappa^{\prime}_{{2}}|1{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\alpha u{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}|{\scriptstyle\!}-{\scriptstyle\!}(\kappa^{\prime}_{{1}}{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}1{\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}\kappa^{\prime}_{{2}}){\scriptstyle\!}+{\scriptstyle\!}O({\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1})^{3}{\scriptstyle\!}>{\scriptstyle\!}0.

(B.14)

Since
g_{0}(\alpha)
is a non-constant polynomial of
\alpha
, we can choose
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying (i) below, then we can choose
\theta,u\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying, where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }g_{0}(\alpha)=1\mbox{ if $k\equiv 0,1,2,7$ ({\rm mod\,}$8$), and \ $g_{0}(\alpha)=-1$ else,}

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\ }\mbox{$\theta=1$ if $(\tilde{\alpha}_{11})_{\rm re}\leq 0$, and $\theta=-1$ else,}

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)\ }u=\sqrt{2}e^{\frac{\theta(1-\delta)\pi{\textbf{{i}}}}{4}},

\displaystyle{\rm(iv)\ }u_{\rm re}=1+O(\delta)^{1},\ \ u_{\rm im}=\theta+O(\delta)^{1},\ \ \ (u_{\rm re})^{2}=(u_{\rm im})^{2}+O(\delta)^{1}=1+O(\delta)^{1},

(B.15)

where (iv) simply follows from (iii).
We can compute from (
B
) to obtain the following,
where (i) follows from (
B
) (i) and the facts that
\theta=\pm 1
and
\delta>0
is sufficiently small, while (ii) is obtained, as
in (
B
), by using (
B
),  (
B
), (iv) and the fact from (
B
) (ii) that
\theta(\tilde{\alpha}_{11})_{\rm re}\geq 0

,

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\rm(i)\ }{\rm C_{oeff}}(C_{1},{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}^{k})=\sqrt{2}\Big(g_{0}(\alpha)e^{\frac{k\theta(1-\delta)\pi{\textbf{{i}}}}{4}}\Big)_{\rm re}=\sqrt{2}g_{0}(\alpha)\cos\Big(\frac{k(1-\delta)\pi{\textbf{{i}}}}{4}\Big)>0,

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)\ }{\rm C_{oeff}}(C_{2},{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}^{2})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\kappa^{\prime}_{1}\big((\tilde{\alpha}_{\rm re})^{2}+(\tilde{\alpha}_{\rm im})^{2}\big)+1+\kappa^{\prime}_{2}\big((\alpha_{\rm re})^{2}+(\alpha_{\rm im})^{2}\big)\Big)-2\theta(\tilde{\alpha}_{11})_{\rm re}+O(\delta)^{1}

\displaystyle\phantom{{\rm(i)\ }{\rm C_{oeff}}(C_{2},{\textbf{{\footnotesize$\mathbf{\mathcal{E}}$}}}_{1}^{2})}\geq\frac{1}{2}+O(\delta)^{1}>0.

(B.16)

Thus
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}>0
by (
B
).
This proves Proposition

.

Appendix C
A proposition provided by Bin Xu

We wish to thank professor Bin Xu from University of Science and Technology of China, who provides us the following
material.

Proposition C.1
.

Assume that
V
satisfies
(
C1
)
–(C3),
(
C4
)^{\prime}
stated in the beginning of section
3
. Then the projection
\pi_{1}:V\to\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
(p_{1},p_{2})\mapsto(x_{1},x_{2})
for
(p_{1},p_{2})\in V
, is not surjective.

For a proof, we need the following lemma in page 7 of
[
4
]
.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
X,Y
be two Hausdorff, locally compact topological spaces, and let
\pi:X\to Y
be both surjective and a local homeomorphism

(
i.e. for any
\alpha\in X
, there exists an open neighbourhood
X_{\alpha}
of
\alpha
such that
Y_{\alpha}=\pi(X_{\alpha})
is open in
Y
and
\pi|_{X_{\alpha}}
is a homeomorphism onto
Y_{\alpha})
. Then
\pi
is a finite covering
(
i.e. for any
y_{0}\in Y
, there is an open neighbourhood
Y_{y_{0}}
of
y_{0}
such that
\pi^{-1}(Y_{y_{0}})
is a disjoint union
\cup_{j=1}^{n}\,X_{j}
of open sets
X_{j}
of
X
with the property that
\pi|_{X_{j}}
is a homeomorphism onto
Y_{y_{0}}
for all
1\leq j\leq n)
if and only if it is proper
(
i.e. for any compact set
K\subset Y
, the inverse image
\pi^{-1}(K)
is compact in
X)
.

Proof of Proposition
C.1
.
Assume that
\pi_{1}
is surjective. By (C3), (C4)
′
,
\pi_{1}
is a local homeomorphism.
By (C2), it is proper (see also Proposition
2.22
). Hence, by Lemma
C.2
,
\pi_{1}
is a covering map. Since
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
is simply connected and
V
is connected,
\pi_{1}
is a holomorphic homeomorphism. Then there exist two holomorphic functions

\phi_{1},\,\phi_{2}
on
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
such

that

V=\Big\{\Big(\big(x_{1},\phi_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\big),\big(x_{2},\phi_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)\Big)\,\Big|\,(x_{1},x_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\Big\}.

By using both (C2) and the Taylor developments of
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
on
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
, we see that

\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
are constant,
which contradicts that in
V
,
x_{1},y_{1}
are locally expressed by holomorphic functions on
x_{2},y_{2}

.
\Box
